(B.26)

\displaystyle\dot{\Theta}_{t}({x})

\displaystyle=-{\nabla}{f}({\Theta}_{t}({x}))\,.

and let
{F}({x})
be the value of this flow at time
1
, i.e.,

(B.27)

{F}({x})={\Theta}_{1}({x})\,.

We first list some basic properties.

Lemma B.4

(Properties of the flow
[
5
, Lem. D.1]
)
.

{\Theta}
is well-defined and continuous in both time and space, and, for any
{T}\geq 0
,
{{\gamma}}\in{\mathcal{C}}([0,{T}],{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}})
such that
{{\gamma}}_{0}={x}
,

(B.28)

{\mathcal{S}}_{0,{T}}({{\gamma}})=0\iff{{\gamma}}_{t}={\Theta}_{t}({x})~~\text{ for all }{t}\in[0,{T}]\,.

The following lemma translates this for
{F}
.

Lemma B.5

(Properties of
{F}
,
[
5
, Lem. D.2]
)
.

{F}
is well-defined and continous and, for any
{x},{{{x}^{\prime}}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
,

(B.29)

{\rho}({x},{{{x}^{\prime}}})=0\iff{{{x}^{\prime}}}={F}({x})\,.

The next two lemmas are key regularity results on the connected components of the critical set.

Lemma B.6

(
[
5
, Lem. D.8]
)
.

For any
{\mathcal{C}}\subset\crit({f})
connected component of the critical set,
there is
{r}_{0}>0
such that, for any
0<{r}\leq{r}_{0}
,

(B.30)

{{{\mathcal{W}}}}_{r}({\mathcal{C}})\coloneqq\{{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}:{\rho}({x},{\mathcal{C}})<{r},\,{\rho}({\mathcal{C}},{x})<{r}\}

is open and contains
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Lemma B.7

(
[
5
, Lem. D.9]
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}\subset\crit({f})
be a connected component of the critical set.
Then, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, there is some
{N}\geq 1
such that, for any
{x},{{z}}\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
there is
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N})
such that
{\xi}_{0}={x}
,
{\xi}_{{N}-1}={{z}}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{N}({\xi})<{\varepsilon}
and
\max_{{0}\leq{n}<{N}}d({\xi}_{n},{\mathcal{C}})<{\varepsilon}
.

B.7.
Convergence and stability

Lemma B.8

(
[
5
, Lem. D.28]
)
.

For any
{x}\in{\mathcal{X}_{\textup{r}}}
, there exists
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
such that

(B.31)

\lim_{{t}\to+\infty}d({\Theta}_{t}({x}),{\mathcal{C}}_{i})=0\,.

Definition 5
.

A connected component of the critical points
{C}\subset\crit({f})
is said to be
asymptotically stable
if there exists
{\mathcal{U}}
a neighborhood of
{C}
such that, for any
{x}\in{\mathcal{U}}
,
{\Theta}_{t}({x})
converges to
{C}
, i.e.,

(B.32)

\lim_{{t}\to+\infty}d({\Theta}_{t}({x}),{C})=0\,.

The notions of minimizing component and asymptotic stability are equivalent in our context.

Definition 6
.

{C}
connected component of
\crit({f})
is
minimizing
if there exists
{\mathcal{U}}
a neighborhood of
{C}
such that

(B.33)

\argmin_{{x}\in{\mathcal{U}}}{f}({x})={C}

Note that since
\crit({f})
is closed,
{C}
is closed as well as a connected component of a closed set.

Lemma B.9

(
[
5
, Lem. D.29]
)
.

For any
{C}\subset\crit({f})
connected component of the set of critical points,

{C}
is minimizing if and only if it is asymptotically stable.

Appendix C
Transitions

In this section, we study the transitions of the accelerated sequence of
SGD
iterates between the different sets of critical points.
As in
[
5
]
, we build on the work of
Kifer [39]
and
Freidlin & Wentzell [22]
.
More precisely,
Sections

C.1
,
C.2
,
C.3
,
C.4
and
C.5

consists in refining the framework of
[
5
]
to be able to obtain precise time estimates on the transitions between the different sets of critical points. Such results are provided in
Section

C.6
.

C.1.
Setup

We adapt to our context
Kifer [39, Lem. 5.4]
and simplify it using ideas from
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6]
.

Definition 7

(
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§2]
)
.

For
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\delta}>0
,

\displaystyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi}):{N}\geq 1,{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}),\,{\xi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i},\,{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},\,{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}\,\text{ for all }{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2\right\}

(C.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi}):{N}\geq 1,{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}),\,{\xi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{i}),\,{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{j}),\,{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}\,\text{ for all }{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2\right\}

While
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}
is the usual definition from
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§2]
,
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}
is a variant that will prove helpful.

Let us now first list a few immediate properties.

Lemma C.1
.

For
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}
is non-decreasing in
{\delta}
and, for any
{\delta}>0
,
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}
.

Note that the fact that
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}
is non-decreasing in
{\delta}
implies that the limit
\lim_{{\delta}\to 0}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}
exists.

We now exploit the regularity around the
{\mathcal{K}}_{i}
, to obtain an alternate expression for
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}
as the limit of
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}
as
{\delta}\to 0
.

Lemma C.2
.

For
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
the following equality holds:

(C.2)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}=\lim_{{\delta}\to 0}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}\,.

Proof.

By
Lemma

C.1
, we have

(C.3)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}\,,

and therefore

(C.4)

\lim_{{\delta}\to 0}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}\,.

It remains to show the reverse inequality.

Take
{\varepsilon}>0
.
Apply
Lemma

B.6
to
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
: at the potential cost of reducing
{\varepsilon}
, we get that
{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
is an open neighborhood of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
.

Take
{\delta}>0
small enough so that
{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})\subset{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
,
{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
does not intersect with any other
{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
,
{l}\neq{j}
.

Now, take any
{N}\geq 1
and
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N})
such that
{\xi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
,
{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{j})
,
{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2
.

By the choice of
{\delta}
,
{\xi}_{0}
cannot be in
\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
and
{\xi}_{0}
is inside
{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
so that there exists
{x}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
such that
{\rho}({x},{\xi}_{0})<{\varepsilon}
.
Similarly, there exists
{{{x}^{\prime}}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
such that
{\rho}({\xi}_{{N}-1},{{{x}^{\prime}}})<{\varepsilon}
.
Now consider the path
{\zeta}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}+2)
defined as
{\zeta}=({x},{\xi}_{0},{\xi}_{1},\dots,{\xi}_{{N}-1},{{{x}^{\prime}}})
that satisfies
{\zeta}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{\zeta}_{{N}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
and
{\zeta}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2
. Furthermore,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}+2}({\zeta})\leq{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi})+2{\varepsilon}
and thus
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}\leq{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi})+2{\varepsilon}
.
Passing to the infimum over such paths
{\xi}
yields

(C.5)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{\delta}+2{\varepsilon}\,,

which concludes the proof.
∎

C.2.
Induced chain

We now define an important object: the law of the (accelerated) iterated at the first time they reach some set
{{\mathcal{V}}}
(typically a neighborhood of the critical set). Due to our interest in the finite-time dynamics, we slightly deviate from the classical definitions of
Douc et al. [18, Chap. 3.4]
and
Kifer [39, Prop. 5.3]
to follow more closely the one of
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§2]
.

Definition 8
.

Consider
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
disjoint neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
and denote by
{\mathcal{V}}\coloneqq\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
their union.
We define recursively the sequences of stopping times
(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
and
(\tau^{{\eta}}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
by

•

For
{n}={0}
,

(C.6)

\displaystyle\sigma^{{\eta}}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{0}

(C.7)

\displaystyle\tau^{{\eta}}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{0}\,.

•

For
{n}\geq{0}
, if
{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}}}
is in
{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}_{{n}}}
, then

(C.8)

\displaystyle\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{n}+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\inf\{{{k}}\geq\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}:{x^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}}\notin{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}_{{n}}}\}&\text{if }{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}_{{n}}}\text{ for some ${i}_{n}$ s.t.~}{\mathcal{C}}_{{i}_{n}}\in\left\{{\mathcal{K}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{K}}_{K}\right\}\,,\\
\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

(C.9)

\displaystyle\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}+1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\inf\{{{k}}\geq\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{n}+1}:{x^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}\}\,.

We denote by
{{z}}_{n}={x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}}}
,
{n}={{0},{1},\dotsc},
the induced Markov chain and by
\probalt_{{\mathcal{V}}}({x},\cdot)
the corresponding Markov transition probability i.e., the law of
{{z}}_{1}
started at
{{z}}_{0}={x}
.

A few remarks are in order on
({{z}}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
:

•

{{z}}_{n}\in{\mathcal{V}}
for all
{n}\geq{1}
.

•

If the chain reaches a neighborhood of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{N_{\text{targ}}}
it stays there forever.

C.3.
Estimates of the transition probabilities

Lemma C.3
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
,
{A}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.10)

\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\leq\begin{cases}\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{if }{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty\,,\\
\exp\left(-\frac{{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

Following the alternative definition of the
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}
’s
Lemma

C.2
, choose
{\delta}>0
small enough so that both

{\mathcal{U}}_{\frac{{\delta}}{2}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
are pairwise disjoints and
the following holds for all
{i},{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
:

(C.11)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}\geq\begin{cases}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-{\varepsilon}\quad&\text{if }{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty\,,\\
{A}\quad&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

Require then that
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
be contained in
{\mathcal{U}}_{\frac{{\delta}}{2}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
for all
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
.
Note that the
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
’s are pairwise disjoint by construction.

Given neighborhoods
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, by compactness of
\crit({f})
, there exists
{{\delta}^{\prime}}>0
such that
{{\delta}^{\prime}}\leq{\frac{{\delta}}{2}}
,
{\mathcal{U}}_{2{{\delta}^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
is contained in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
for all
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
.

Fix
{i},{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
and consider
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N})
such that
{\xi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}}_{i})\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
,
{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}}_{j})\subset{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j})
and
{\xi}_{n}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}\left({{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}\setminus\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{V}}_{l}\right)
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2
.
By the choice of
{{\delta}^{\prime}}
,
{\xi}_{n}
cannot be in
\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{C}}_{l})
for any
{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2
.

By construction of
{\xi}
we thus have that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi})

\displaystyle\geq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}^{{\delta}}

(C.12)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-{\varepsilon}&\text{if }{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty\,,\\
{A}&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

where the last inequality follows from
Eq.

C.11
.

Fix
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
. Let us now bound the probability

(C.13)

\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})=\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\right)\,,

and start with the case where
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty
.

We have, for any
{N}\geq{0}
,

(C.14)

\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\right)\leq\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\,\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}\right)+\prob_{x}\left(\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}\geq{N}\right)\,.

We first bound the second probability using
Lemma

B.3
applied to
{\mathcal{U}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
Take
{N}
such that
{\alpha}_{0}({a}-{N})+{\eta}_{0}{b}\leq-{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}
.
Then, by Markov’s inequality and
Lemma

B.3
, it holds that for all
{\eta}\leq{\eta}_{0}

\displaystyle\prob_{x}\left(\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}\geq{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prob_{x}\left(\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{0}\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}{{\eta}}\right)\geq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{0}{N}}{{\eta}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{0}({a}-{N})}{{\eta}}+{b}\right)

(C.15)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{-{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

We now bound the term
\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j},\,\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}\right)
for this choice of
{N}
.

For this, we show that
{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
with
\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}
implies that

(C.16)

\dist_{{N}}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{{\Gamma}}_{{N}}^{\{{x}\}}\left({{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-2{\varepsilon}\right)\right)>{\frac{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}{2}}\,.

Indeed, on the event
{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
with
\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}
, there is some
{{N}^{\prime}}\leq{N}
such that
\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}={{N}^{\prime}}-1
.
If
\dist_{{N}}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{{\Gamma}}_{{N}}^{\{{x}\}}\left({{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-2{\varepsilon}\right)\right)>{\frac{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}{2}}
did not hold, this would mean that there exists
{\xi}\in({{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}})^{{{N}^{\prime}}}
such that
\dist_{{{N}^{\prime}}}({x^{{\eta}}},{\xi})<{{\delta}^{\prime}}
,
{\xi}_{0}={x}
and,

{\mathcal{A}}_{{{N}^{\prime}}}({\xi})\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-2{\varepsilon}
. In particular,
{\xi}
would also satisfy

{\xi}_{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{V}}_{j})
,
{\xi}_{n}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}({{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}\setminus{\mathcal{V}})
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{{N}^{\prime}}-2
and, as a consequence,
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({{N}^{\prime}})
.
This would be thus in direct contradiction of
Eq.

C.12
.

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j},\,\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prob_{x}\left(\dist_{{N}}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{{\Gamma}}_{{N}}^{\{{x}\}}\left({{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-2{\varepsilon}\right)\right)>{\frac{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}{2}}\right)

(C.17)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-3{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

by
Proposition

B.1
.

Combining this bound with
Eq.

C.15
yields

(C.18)

\prob_{x}({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}-3{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)+\exp\left(\frac{-{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

which concludes the proof when
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty
.

Let us now examine the case where
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}=+\infty
.
Again, we have, for any
{N}\geq{0}
,

(C.19)

\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\right)\leq\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i},\,\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}\right)+\prob_{x}\left(\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}\geq{N}\right)\,.

We first bound the second probability using
Lemma

B.3
applied to
{\mathcal{U}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
Take
{N}
such that
{\alpha}_{0}({a}-{N})+{\eta}_{0}{b}\leq-{A}
.
Then, by Markov’s inequality and
Lemma

B.3
, it holds that for all
{\eta}\leq{\eta}_{0}

\displaystyle\prob_{x}\left(\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}\geq{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prob_{x}\left(\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{0}\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}{{\eta}}\right)\geq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{0}{N}}{{\eta}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}_{0}({a}-{N})}{{\eta}}+{b}\right)

(C.20)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{-{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

We now bound the term
\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j},\,\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}\right)
for this choice of
{N}
.

For this, we show that
{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
with
\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}
implies that

(C.21)

\dist_{{N}}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{{\Gamma}}_{{N}}^{\{{x}\}}\left({A}-{\varepsilon}\right)\right)>{\frac{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}{2}}\,.

Indeed, on the event
{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
with
\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}
, there is some
{{N}^{\prime}}\leq{N}
such that
\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}={{N}^{\prime}}-1
.
If
\dist_{{N}}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{{\Gamma}}_{{N}}^{\{{x}\}}\left({A}-{\varepsilon}\right)\right)>{\frac{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}{2}}
did not hold, this would mean that there exists
{\xi}\in({{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}})^{{{N}^{\prime}}}
such that
\dist_{{{N}^{\prime}}}({x^{{\eta}}},{\xi})<{{\delta}^{\prime}}
,
{\xi}_{0}={x}
and,

{\mathcal{A}}_{{{N}^{\prime}}}({\xi})\leq{A}-{\varepsilon}
. In particular,
{\xi}
would also satisfy

{\xi}_{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{V}}_{j})
,
{\xi}_{n}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}({{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}\setminus{\mathcal{V}})
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{{N}^{\prime}}-2
and, as a consequence,
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({{N}^{\prime}})
.
This would be thus in direct contradiction of
Eq.

C.12
.

Therefore, we have that

\displaystyle\prob_{x}\left({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j},\,\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}<{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prob_{x}\left(\dist_{{N}}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{{\Gamma}}_{{N}}^{\{{x}\}}\left({A}-{\varepsilon}\right)\right)>{\frac{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}{2}}\right)

(C.22)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{A}-2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

by
Proposition

B.1
.

Combining this bound with
Eq.

C.20
yields

(C.23)

\prob_{x}({x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{\mathcal{V}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{j})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{A}-2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)+\exp\left(\frac{-{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

which concludes the proof.

∎

Lemma C.4
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
,for any neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.24)

\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

Note that this result is trivially valid if
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}=+\infty
.

Proof.

For any
({i},{j})\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\times{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
, there exists
{N}_{{i},{j}}\geq 1
,
{\xi}^{{i},{j}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}_{{i},{j}})
such that
{\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{{N}_{{i},{j}}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
,
{\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{N}_{{i},{j}}-2
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}_{{i},{j}}}({\xi}^{{i},{j}})\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}+{\varepsilon}
.

Define
{\delta}_{{i},{j}}\coloneqq\min\left\{d({\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{n},\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}):{n}=1,\dots,{N}_{{i},{j}}-2\right\}
and
{\delta}\coloneqq\min_{{i},{j}\in{I}}{\delta}_{{i},{j}}
. By construction, it holds that
{\delta}>0
.

Without loss of generality, at the potential cost of reducing
{\varepsilon}
, we can assume that
Lemma

B.6
can be applied to every
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{K}
with
{r}\leftarrow{\varepsilon}
and denote by
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
the corresponding neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
.
Require that
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
be contained in
{\mathcal{W}}_{i}\cap{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}/2}({\mathcal{K}}_{i})
for all
{i}=1,\dots,{{K}}
.
Now, given such
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, by compactness, there exists
0<{{\delta}^{\prime}}\leq{\delta}/2
such that
{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}^{\prime}}({\mathcal{C}}_{i})
is contained in
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
for all
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
.

Apply
Lemma

B.7
to
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{{K}}
with
{\varepsilon}\leftarrow\min({\varepsilon},{{\delta}^{\prime}}/2)
and denote by
{N}_{i}
the bound on the length of paths obtained.

We are now ready to prove the result.
Fix
({i},{j})\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\times{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
and consider
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
.
Since
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}\subset{\mathcal{W}}_{i}
, there exists
{z}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{i}
such that
{\rho}({x},{z})<{\varepsilon}
.

By
Lemma

B.7
, there exists
{n}\leq{N}
,
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({n})
such that
{\xi}_{0}={z}
,
{\xi}_{{N}-1}={\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{0}
,
{\xi}_{{k}}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}/2}({\mathcal{K}}_{i})
for all
{{k}}=1,\dots,{n}-2
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{n}({\xi})<{\varepsilon}
.

Considering the concatenation

(C.25)

{\zeta}\coloneqq\left({x},{\xi}_{0},{\xi}_{1},\dots,{\xi}_{{n}-2},{\xi}_{{n}-1},{\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{1},\dots,{\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{{N}_{{i},{j}}-1}\right)

which is a path of length
{n}+{N}_{{i},{j}}+1
made of
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
, then exactly
{n}
points in
{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}/2}({\mathcal{K}}_{i})
then
{N}_{{i},{j}}-2
in
{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}\setminus{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}/2}({\mathcal{V}})
and
{\xi}^{{i},{j}}_{{N}_{{i},{j}}-1}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{j}
.
Moreover, by construction,
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}+{N}_{{i},{j}}}({\zeta})\leq{\widetilde{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}+3{\varepsilon}
.
Therefore, if

(C.26)

\dist_{{n}+{N}_{{i},{j}}+1}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{\zeta}\right)<{{\delta}^{\prime}}/2\,,

with
{x^{{\eta}}_{{0}}}={x}
, then
{x^{{\eta}}_{1}},\dots,{x^{{\eta}}_{{n}}}
are in
{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}}({\mathcal{K}}_{i})\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
and, since
{{\delta}^{\prime}}\leq{\delta}/2
,
{x^{{\eta}}_{{n}+2}},\dots,{x^{{\eta}}_{{n}+{N}_{{i},{j}}-1}}
are not in
{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}/4}({\mathcal{V}})
, and therefore not in
{\mathcal{V}}
. Moreover,
{x^{{\eta}}_{{n}+{N}_{{i},{j}}}}
would be in
{\mathcal{U}}_{{{\delta}^{\prime}}/2}({\mathcal{K}}_{j})\subset{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
.

Thus, all the paths
{x^{{\eta}}}
satisfying (
C.26
) with
{x^{{\eta}}_{{0}}}={x}
correspond to exactly one transition of the induced chain
({{z}}_{n})_{{n}}
from
{x}
to
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}}
.

Therefore, using the definition of
\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle\geq\prob_{x}\left(\dist_{{n}+{N}_{{i},{j}}+1}\left({x^{{\eta}}},{\zeta}\right)<{{\delta}^{\prime}}/2\right)

(C.27)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}+4{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

by
Proposition

B.1
.
∎

C.4.
Accelerated induced chain

We will take the following convention, for any
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\delta}>0
:

(C.28)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{i}}^{{\delta}}={{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{i}}={{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{i}}=0\,.

Recall that
\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}
is the transition probabilities of the induced chain
({{z}}_{n})_{{n}\geq 0}
, defined in
Definition

8
. In other words
\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{\mathcal{V}}_{j})
is the probability that the sequence of
Eq.

B.16
starting from
{x}
at time
0
enters
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
before any other
{\mathcal{V}}_{l}
for
{l}\neq{j}
.
Moreover,
{K}
still denotes the number of connected components of the critical set of
{f}
that are not part of the global minimum, see
Definition

1
in
Section

B.2
.

Definition 9
.

Given the induced chain
({{z}}_{n})_{{n}\geq 0}
, we define the accelerated induced chain
({{{z}}^{{K}}}_{n})_{{n}\geq 0}
as follows:

(C.29)

{{{z}}^{{K}}}_{{n}}\coloneqq{{z}}_{{n}{K}}\,.

We denote by
\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}
the transition probabilities of the accelerated induced chain which corresponds to the
{K}-th
power of
\probalt_{{\mathcal{V}}}
: it satisfies
12
12

12

This equation is sometimes referred to as the Chapman-Kolmogorov equation.
, for any
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
and any measurable set
A\subset{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
,

(C.30)

\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},A)=\int\int\dots\int\one\{{{z}}_{{K}}\in A\}\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},d{{z}}_{1})\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({{z}}_{1},d{{z}}_{2})\dots\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({{z}}_{{K}-1},d{{z}}_{{K}})\,.

We now define transition costs for the accelerated induced chain, which correspond the ones introduced in the main text
Eq.

19
in
Section

4
.

Definition 10

(Inspired by
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§2]
)
.

For
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\delta}>0
,

(C.31)

\displaystyle{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi}):{N}\geq 1,{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}),\,{\xi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i},\,{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},\,{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j},\,{l}>{K}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}\,\text{ for all }{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2\right\}

Lemma C.5
.

For any
({i},{j})\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\times{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,

(C.32)

\displaystyle{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\sum_{{l}=0}^{{n}-2}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{l}},{i}_{{l}+1}}:{i}_{0}={i},\,{i}_{{n}-1}={j},\,{i}_{{l}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\text{ for }{l}=1,\dots,{n}-2\,,{n}\geq 1\right\}

(C.33)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\sum_{{l}=0}^{{{K}}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{l}},{i}_{{l}+1}}:{i}_{0}={i},\,{i}_{{{K}}}={j},\,{i}_{{l}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\text{ for }{l}=1,\dots,{{K}}-1\right\}\,.

Proof.

We focus on proving the first equality for
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
; the proof for
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
follows similarly.
Let us denote by
{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})
the right-hand side:

(C.34)

{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})\coloneqq\min\left\{\sum_{{l}=0}^{{n}-2}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{l}},{i}_{{l}+1}}:{i}_{0}={i},\,{i}_{{n}-1}={j},\,{i}_{{l}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\text{ for }{l}=1,\dots,{n}-2\,,{n}\geq 1\right\}

We will prove that
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}={\text{RHS}}({i},{j})
.

(
\geq
): Let us show that
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}\geq{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})
. Fix
{\varepsilon}>0
. There is a path
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N})
with
{\xi}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
that avoids
\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j},\,{l}>{K}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
so that, by definition of
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
(
Definition

10
),

(C.35)

{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}\geq{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi})-{\varepsilon}\,.

Let
1\leq{n}_{1}<{n}_{2}<\dots<{n}_{{N}^{\prime}}<{N}
be the times when
{\xi}
belongs to some
{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
with
{l}\leq{K}
. Define
{i}_{0}={i}
,
{i}_{{N}^{\prime}}={j}
and for
1\leq i<{{N}^{\prime}}
, let
{i}_{{k}}
be such that
{\xi}_{{n}_{{k}}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{i}_{{k}}}
. Then, by definition of
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}
(
Definition

7
),

(C.36)

{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi})\geq\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}\,.

Combining
Eq.

C.35
and
Eq.

C.36
and taking yields

(C.37)

\displaystyle{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}-{\varepsilon}

(C.38)

\displaystyle\geq{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})-{\varepsilon}\,,

since this sequence
({i}_{{k}})_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}}
satisfies
{i}_{0}={i}
,
{i}_{{N}^{\prime}}={j}
and
{i}_{{k}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
for
1\leq{{k}}<{{N}^{\prime}}
.

(
\leq
): Let us show that
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}\leq{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})
. Take
{\varepsilon}>0
and let
({i}_{{k}})_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}}
be a sequence achieving the minimum in
{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})
up to
{\varepsilon}
, i.e.,

(C.39)

\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}\leq{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})+{\varepsilon}\,.

Take
{\delta}>0
small enough such that
{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}}({\mathcal{C}}_{l})
,
{l}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
are pairwise disjoint.

For each
0\leq{{k}}<{{N}^{\prime}}
, by definition of
{{{\mathcal{B}}}}
(
Definition

7
), there exists
{\delta}_{{k}}\in(0,{\delta})
and a path
{\xi}^{{k}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}_{{k}})
such that: (i)
{\xi}^{{k}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{i}_{{k}}}
, (ii)
{\xi}^{{k}}_{{N}_{{k}}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{i}_{{{k}}+1}}
, (iii)
{\xi}^{{k}}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{N}_{{k}}-2
, and (iv)
{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}_{{k}}}({\xi}^{{k}})\leq{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}+{\varepsilon}/{{N}^{\prime}}
.

By
Lemma

B.7
, for each
0\leq{{k}}<{{N}^{\prime}}-1
, there exists
\tilde{{\xi}}^{{k}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}(\tilde{{N}}_{{k}})
such that: (i)
\tilde{{\xi}}^{{k}}_{0}={\xi}^{{k}}_{{N}_{{k}}-1}
, (ii)
\tilde{{\xi}}^{{k}}_{\tilde{{N}}_{{k}}-1}={\xi}^{{{k}}+1}_{0}
, (iii)
\tilde{{\xi}}^{{k}}_{n}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}/2}({\mathcal{C}}_{{i}_{{{k}}+1}})
and therefore
\tilde{{\xi}}^{{k}}_{n}\not\in\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i}_{{{k}}+1}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
for all
{n}=1,\dots,\tilde{{N}}_{{k}}-2
,
and (iv)
{\mathcal{A}}_{\tilde{{N}}_{{k}}}(\tilde{{\xi}}^{{k}})\leq{\varepsilon}/{{N}^{\prime}}
.

Concatenating the paths
{\xi}^{0},\tilde{{\xi}}^{0},{\xi}^{1},\tilde{{\xi}}^{1},\dots,{\xi}^{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}
yields a path
{\zeta}
that: (i) starts in
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
, (ii) ends in
{\mathcal{C}}_{j}
, (iii) avoids
\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j},\,{l}>{K}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
, and (iv) has total cost at most
\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}+3{\varepsilon}
.
Therefore, by definition of
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
(
Definition

10
),

\displaystyle{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{{N}^{\prime}}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}+3{\varepsilon}

(C.40)

\displaystyle\leq{\text{RHS}}({i},{j})+4{\varepsilon}\,.

The second equality in each case follows from the fact that optimal paths between different components can be chosen without cycles (since all costs are non-negative), and therefore their length can be bounded by the number of components
{K}
.

∎

Lemma C.6

(Upper bound on accelerated induced chain transition probability)
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
,
{A}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.41)

\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\leq\begin{cases}\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{if }{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty\,,\\
\exp\left(-\frac{{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

Proof.

Fix
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
. By
Lemma

C.5
, we have that

(C.42)

{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}=\min\left\{\sum_{{l}=0}^{{K}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{l}},{i}_{{l}+1}}:{i}_{0}={i},\,{i}_{{K}}={j},\,{i}_{{l}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\text{ for }{l}=1,\dots,{K}-1\right\}\,.

First, let us consider the case where
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty
. Take
{\varepsilon}>0
and let
({i}_{{k}})_{{{k}}=0}^{{K}}
be a sequence achieving the minimum in
Eq.

C.42
up to
{\varepsilon}
, i.e.,

(C.43)

\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{K}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}\leq{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}+{\varepsilon}\,.

By definition of the accelerated induced chain (
Definition

9
), we have that for any
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,

(C.44)

\displaystyle\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{i}_{1},\dots,{i}_{{K}-1}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}}\int_{{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}_{1}}}\cdots\int_{{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}_{{K}-1}}}\prod_{{{k}}=0}^{{K}-1}\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x}_{{k}},d{x}_{{{k}}+1})\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x}_{{K}},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\,,

where we define
{x}_{0}={x}
.

Therefore, combining
Eq.

C.44
with

Lemma

C.3
and
Eq.

C.43
, we obtain that for any
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,

\displaystyle\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{i}_{1},\dots,{i}_{{K}-1}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}}\prod_{{{k}}=0}^{{K}-1}\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{k}},{i}_{{{k}}+1}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}{K}}\right)

(C.45)

\displaystyle\leq{K}^{{K}-1}\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

For the case where
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}=+\infty
, by the same reasoning but using the alternative bound from
Lemma

C.3
, we get

(C.46)

\displaystyle\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle\leq{K}^{{K}-1}\exp\left(-\frac{{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

Taking
{\eta}_{0}
small enough so that
{K}^{{K}-1}\leq\exp({\varepsilon}/{\eta})
for all
{\eta}\leq{\eta}_{0}
, the result follows from
Eqs.

C.45
and
C.46
.
∎

Lemma C.7

(Lower bound on accelerated induced chain transition probability)
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.47)

\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

The proof of
Lemma

C.7
is very similar to the proof of
Lemma

C.6
and is therefore omitted.

We end this section by restating a result from
[
5
]
that provides sufficient conditions for
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
to be finite for any
({i},{j})\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\times{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
.

Lemma C.8

(
[
5
, Lem. D.12]
)
.

Consider
{x},{{{x}^{\prime}}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
and assume that there exists
{T}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{{\gamma}}\in\mathcal{C}^{1}([0,{T}],{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}})
such that
{{\gamma}}_{0}={x}
,
{{\gamma}}_{T}={{{x}^{\prime}}}
,
{{\gamma}}_{n}\not\in\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j},\,{l}>{K}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}
for all
{n}=1,\dots,{T}-1
and,
for every
{t}\in[0,{T}]
,
{\nabla}{f}({{\gamma}}_{t})
is in the interior of the closed convex hull of the support of
{u}({{\gamma}}_{t},{\omega})
, i.e.,

(C.48)

{\nabla}{f}({{\gamma}}_{t})\in\intr\clconv\supp{u}({{\gamma}}_{t},{\omega})\,.

Then,
{\mathcal{B}}({x},{{{x}^{\prime}}})<+\infty
.

C.5.
Initial transition

Definition 11

(Inspired by
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§2]
)
.

For
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\delta}>0
,
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
,

(C.49)

\displaystyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi}):{N}\geq 1,{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}),\,{\xi}_{0}\in{x},\,{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},\,{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}\,\text{ for all }{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2\right\}

(C.50)

\displaystyle{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}^{{\delta}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi}):{N}\geq 1,{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}),\,{\xi}_{0}={x},\,{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{U}}_{\delta}({\mathcal{C}}_{j}),\,{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{i},{j}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}\,\text{ for all }{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2\right\}\,.

The proof of the following lemmas are very similar to the ones of
Lemmas

C.2
,
C.3
and
C.4
and are therefore omitted.

Lemma C.9
.

For any
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\delta}>0
,
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
,

(C.51)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}=\lim_{{\delta}\to 0}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}^{{\delta}}\,.

Lemma C.10
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
,
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
, for any neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.52)

\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}}{{\eta}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

Lemma C.11
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
,
{A}>0
,
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.53)

\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\leq\begin{cases}\exp\left(-\frac{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{if }{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{j}}<+\infty\,,\\
\exp\left(-\frac{{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

Following the same methodology as for transitions between critical components, we now establish similar results for transitions starting from an arbitrary initial state.

Definition 12

(Inspired by
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§2]
)
.

For
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\delta}>0
,

(C.54)

\displaystyle{{{B}}}_{{x},{j}}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\mathcal{A}}_{{N}}({\xi}):{N}\geq 1,{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N}),\,{\xi}_{0}={x},\,{\xi}_{{N}-1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{j},\,{\xi}_{n}\notin\bigcup_{{l}\neq{j},\,{l}>{K}}{\mathcal{C}}_{l}\,\text{ for all }{n}=1,\dots,{N}-2\right\}

This definition mirrors the one of
Definition

10
, adapting it to account for an arbitrary initial state rather than starting from a critical component. We can then establish the following decomposition result, analogous to
Lemma

C.5
.

Lemma C.12
.

For any
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,

(C.55)

\displaystyle{{{B}}}_{{x},{j}}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{i}_{1}}+\sum_{{l}=1}^{{n}-2}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{l}},{i}_{{l}+1}}:{i}_{{n}-1}={j},\,{i}_{{l}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\text{ for }{l}=1,\dots,{n}-2,\,{n}\geq 1\right\}

(C.56)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{x},{i}_{1}}+\sum_{{l}=1}^{{K}-1}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i}_{{l}},{i}_{{l}+1}}:{i}_{{K}}={j},\,{i}_{{l}}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}\text{ for }{l}=1,\dots,{K}-1\right\}

The proof follows the same arguments as in
Lemma

C.5
, replacing the initial component with the given initial state.

These structural results allow us to establish bounds on the transition probabilities from an arbitrary initial state, paralleling those of
Lemmas

C.6
and
C.7
.

Lemma C.13

(Upper bound on accelerated induced chain initial transition probability)
.

For any
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
,
{A}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.57)

\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\leq\begin{cases}\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{x},{j}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{if }{{{B}}}_{{x},{j}}<+\infty\,,\\
\exp\left(-\frac{{A}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

Lemma C.14

(Lower bound on accelerated induced chain initial transition probability)
.

For any
{x}\in{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(C.58)

\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{x},{j}}}{{\eta}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

The proofs of these last two lemmas follow very closely those of
Lemmas

C.6
and
C.7
, with the main modification being the treatment of the initial state instead of an initial component. The key arguments involving the Chapman-Kolmogorov equation and the handling of the intermediate transitions remain essentially unchanged.

C.6.
Transition time

Let us begin with a preliminary escape result.

Lemma C.15
.

Given
{C}
a connected components of
{{\mathcal{K}}_{{\text{crit}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\eta}>0
small enough and
{\mathcal{V}}
a small enough neighborhood of
{C}
, it holds that, for any
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}
,

(C.59)

\ex_{{x}}\left[\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\right]\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

Proof.

Fix
{\varepsilon}>1
.
By
Lemma

B.6
,
{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({C})
is an open neighborhood of
{C}
.
Since
{C}
is closed,
{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({C})\setminus{C}
is not empty and open. Therefore, there exists
{x}_{3}\in{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({C})\setminus{C}
and
{\delta}>0
such that
{\mathbb{B}}({x}_{3},{\delta})\subset{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({C})\setminus{C}
.
By construction, there exists
{x}_{2}\in{C}
such that
{\rho}({x}_{2},{x}_{3})<{\varepsilon}
.

Let us now apply
Lemma

B.7
to obtain that there exists
{N}\geq 2
such that, for any
{x}_{1}\in{C}
, there exists
{n}\leq{N}
,
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({N})
such that
{\xi}_{0}={x}_{1}
,
{\xi}_{{N}-1}={x}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{n}({\xi})<{\varepsilon}
.

Require then that
{\mathcal{V}}
be contained in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({C})\setminus{\overline{\mathbb{B}}}({x}_{3},{\delta}/2)
which is an open neighborhood of
{C}
.

Take
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}
. Since
{\mathcal{V}}
is in particular contained in
{\mathcal{W}}_{\varepsilon}({C})
, there exists
{x}_{1}\in{C}
such that
{\rho}({x},{x}_{1})<{\varepsilon}
.
By our application of
Lemma

B.7
above, there exists
{n}\leq{N}
,
{\xi}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({n})
such that
{\xi}_{0}={x}_{1}
,
{\xi}_{{n}-1}={x}_{2}
,
{\mathcal{A}}_{n}({\xi})<{\varepsilon}
.
Now consider the path
{\zeta}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{r}({n}+2)
defined by

(C.60)

{\zeta}=({x},{\xi}_{0},{\xi}_{1},\dots,{\xi}_{{n}-1},{x}_{3})\,,

which satisfies

(C.61)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{{n}+3}({\zeta})

\displaystyle={\rho}({x},{x}_{1})+{\mathcal{A}}_{{n}}({\xi})+{\rho}({x}_{2},{x}_{3})<3{\varepsilon}\,.

If a trajectory of
SGD

{x^{{\eta}}}
with
{{x^{{\eta}}}}_{{0}}={x}
satisfies
\dist_{{n}+2}({x^{{\eta}}},{\zeta})<{\delta}/2
, then
{x^{{\eta}}_{{n}+1}}
is inside
{\mathbb{B}}({x}_{3},{\delta}/2)
and therefore not in
{\mathcal{V}}
.

Hence, we have, for
{\eta}>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\prob_{{x}}(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}<{N}+3)

\displaystyle\geq\prob_{{x}}(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}<{n}+3)

\displaystyle\geq\prob_{{x}}(\dist_{{n}+2}({x^{{\eta}}},{\zeta})<{\delta}/2)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{\mathcal{A}}_{{n}+2}({\zeta})+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)

(C.62)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{4{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

where we invoked
Proposition

B.1

and used
Eq.

C.61
.

For any
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}
, for
{n}\geq 2
, the (weak) Markov property yields that

\displaystyle\prob_{x}\left(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{n}({N}+2)\right)

\displaystyle=\ex_{{x}}\left[\one\{\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{N}+2\}\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{{N}+2}}}\left[\one\{\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{N}+2\}\dots\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{({n}-2)({N}+1)}}}\left[\one\{\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{N}+2\}\right]\dots\right]\right]

(C.63)

\displaystyle\leq\left(2-\exp\left(-\frac{4{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\right)^{n}\,,

with
Eq.

C.62
.

We can now finally estimate the expectation of
\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}
: for
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}
,

\displaystyle\ex_{{x}}\left[\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{n}=1}^{\infty}\prob_{{x}}(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{{n}=1}^{\infty}\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{N}}\prob_{{x}}(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{n}({N}+1)+{{k}})

\displaystyle\leq({N}+2)\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\prob_{{x}}(\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}>{n}({N}+1))

\displaystyle\leq({N}+2)\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\left(1-\exp\left(-\frac{4{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\right)^{n}

(C.64)

\displaystyle=({N}+2)\exp\left(\frac{4{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

where we used
Eq.

C.63
.

Finally, taking
{\eta}>0
small enough so that
{N}+1\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)
yields that

(C.65)

\ex_{{x}}\left[\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}\right]\leq\exp\left(\frac{5{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.16
.

For any
{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\subset{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
compact,
for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\setminus\bigcup_{{i}={K}+1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
,

(C.66)

1\leq\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}]\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

Let us begin with the
LHS
(
LHS
) inequality. There are two cases: either
{x}
belongs to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
or not.
If
{x}
belongs to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
, then by definition
\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}\geq 1
and therefore
\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}\geq 1
.
If
{x}
does not belong to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
, then
\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}=0
but necessarily
\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}\geq 1
.
Hence, in all cases,
\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}\geq 1
so that the
LHS
inequality holds.

We now turn to the
RHS
(
RHS
) inequality.
As before, we will separate the proof into two cases: either
{x}
belongs to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
or not.
If
{x}
belongs to some
{\mathcal{V}}_{i}
for some
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
then, by
Lemma

C.15
, we have that

(C.67)

\ex_{{x}}[\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}]=\ex_{{x}}[\sigma^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}_{i}}]\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

In particular,
\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}
is finite almost surely.
Therefore, the strong Markov property implies that

(C.68)

\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}]=\ex_{{x}}[\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}]+\ex_{{x}}[\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}}}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}]]\,.

Applying
Lemma

B.3
with
{\mathcal{U}}\leftarrow\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
and
{{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{X}_{0}}
,
and using Jensen’s inequality, we obtain that

(C.69)

\displaystyle\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{\sigma^{{\eta}}_{1}}}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{V}}}]\leq{a}+\frac{{b}{\eta}_{0}}{{\alpha}_{0}}\eqqcolon{c}\,.

Plugging this bound into
Eq.

C.68
and using
Eq.

C.67
yields

(C.70)

\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}]\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}+{c}\,,

which concludes the proof of this case.

Finally, if
{x}
does not belong to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
, then one only needs to apply
Lemma

B.3
as above to obtain the result.
∎

Corollary C.1
.

For any
{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\subset{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
compact,
for any
{N}\geq 1
,
for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\setminus\bigcup_{{i}={K}+1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
,

(C.71)

1\leq\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{N}}]\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

The lower bound follows directly from the definition of
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{N}}
since
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{k}}\geq{{k}}
for all
{{k}}\geq 1
by construction.

For the upper bound, first note that we can write
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{N}}
as a telescoping sum:

(C.72)

\tau^{{\eta}}_{{N}}=\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{N}-1}(\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}+1}-\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}})\,,

with the convention that
\tau^{{\eta}}_{0}=0
. Therefore,

(C.73)

\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{N}}]=\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{{N}-1}\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}+1}-\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}]\,.

By the strong Markov property applied at time
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}
for each term, we have

\displaystyle\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}+1}-\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}]

\displaystyle=\ex_{{x}}[\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}}}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}]]

(C.74)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,,

where the inequality follows from
Lemma

C.16
since
{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}}}
belongs to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
by definition of
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{{k}}}
for all
{{k}}\geq 1
(and for
{{k}}=0
, we can apply
Lemma

C.16
directly to the initial point
{x}
).

Summing over
{{k}}
from
0
to
{N}-1
yields

\displaystyle\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{N}}]

\displaystyle\leq{N}e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}

(C.75)

\displaystyle\leq e^{\frac{2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,,

for
{\eta}
small enough.
∎

Appendix D
Finite-Time Analysis

D.1.
Markov Chains on finite state spaces

In this subsection, we introduce the necessary notation and then restate a key lemma from
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§3]
.

Consider a finite set
{\mathcal{V}}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}\subseteq{\mathcal{V}}
.
Denote by
{r}\coloneqq\card{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
.

Given
{p}:({\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}})\times{\mathcal{V}}\to[0,1]
, we define the probability of a
{g}
with edges in
({\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}})\times{\mathcal{V}}
as

(D.1)

{\pi}\left({g},{p}\right)\coloneqq\prod_{{i}\to{j}\in{g}}{p}({i}\to{j})\,.

A graph
{g}
consisting of arrows
{j}\to{k}
with
{j}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
,
{k}\in{\mathcal{V}}
,
{j}\neq{k}
is called a
{\mathcal{Q}}
-graph on
{\mathcal{V}}
if

(i)

Every vertex in
{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
has exactly one outgoing arrow.

(ii)

There are no cycles, or, equivalently, from every vertex in
{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
there is a directed path to a vertex in
{\mathcal{Q}}
.

The set of
{\mathcal{Q}}
-graphs is denoted by
{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{Q}})
.

We denote by
{\mathcal{G}}({i}\not\leadsto{\mathcal{Q}})
the set of
graphs with exactly
\card({\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}})-1
edges from
{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
to
{\mathcal{V}}
, no cycles and no path from
{i}
to
{\mathcal{Q}}
.
Note that, equivalently, this set is made of all
{\mathcal{Q}}
-graphs from which a single edge from the path from
{i}
to
{\mathcal{Q}}
has been removed.

Lemma D.1

(
Freidlin & Wentzell [22, Chap. 6,§3,Lem. 3.4]
)
.

Consider a Markov Chain on state space
{X}=\bigcup_{{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}}{X}_{{i}}
with
{X}_{{i}},{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}
disjoint and non-empty, with transition probabilities that satisfy:
for
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
,
{j}\in{\mathcal{V}}
with
{i}\neq{j}
,

(D.2)

\displaystyle\forall{x}\in{X}_{{i}},\quad{a}^{-1}{{p}}_{{i}{j}}\leq\prob({x},{X}_{j})\leq{a}{{p}}_{{i}{j}}\,,

for some
{a}\geq 1
,
{{p}}_{{i}{j}}>0
.
For
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
, the expected time to reach
{\mathcal{Q}}
when starting at
{x}
denoted by
{m}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}({x})
satisfies: for all
{x}\in{X}_{{i}}
,

(D.3)

{a}^{3\times 4^{{r}}}\frac{\sum_{{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}({i}\not\leadsto{\mathcal{Q}})}{\pi}\left({g},{\overline{{p}}}\right)}{\sum_{{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}}{\pi}\left({g},{\overline{{p}}}\right)}\leq{m}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}({x})\leq{a}^{3\times 4^{{r}}}\frac{\sum_{{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}({i}\not\leadsto{\mathcal{Q}})}{\pi}\left({g},{\underline{{p}}}\right)}{\sum_{{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}}{\pi}\left({g},{\underline{{p}}}\right)}

D.2.
Hitting time of the accelerated process

We will instantiate the lemmas of the previous section
Section

D.1
with
{\mathcal{V}}={\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{\mathcal{Q}}=\{{K}+1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}
and
{X}_{{i}}={\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
for
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
. We have
{r}={N_{{\text{crit}}}}-{K}
.
Note that the notation
{\mathcal{Q}}
is in line with the notation of the main text where we denote by
{\mathcal{Q}}
both the set of indices
\{{K}+1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}
and the set of union of the corresponding components

\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{C}}_{{j}}=\bigcup_{{j}=1}^{{N_{\text{targ}}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{{j}}=\argmin{f}
.

Define by
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\coloneqq\tau^{{\eta}}_{\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}}
the hitting time of
\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}
for the accelerated
SGD
process.
We also consider the induced chain
({{z}}_{{n}})_{{n}\geq{0}}
on
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
defined in
Definition

8
as well as its accelerated version defined in
Definition

9

and denote by
\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
the hitting time of
\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}
for this accelerated induced chain.
These two hitting times are related by the following lemma, which is a key consequence of
Lemma

C.16
.

Lemma D.2
.

For any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
,

(D.4)

\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\times e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

First, if
{x}
belongs to
\bigcup_{{i}={K}+1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}=\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}
, then
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}=0
and
\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}=0
so the statement holds trivially.

We now consider the case where
{x}
belongs to
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
. Since
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{K}
are compact, we can require that
\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
be relatively compact.
We can now apply
Corollary

C.1
to the accelerated process to obtain that with
{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\leftarrow\cl{\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}}
, for any small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there is some
{\eta}_{0}>0
such for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
, we have,

(D.5)

1\leq\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}]\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

We now have that, by
Definition

8
,

\displaystyle\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]

\displaystyle=\ex_{{x}}\left[\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\one\big\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}{K}}}}\notin\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}\big\}\left(\tau^{{\eta}}_{({n}+1){K}}-\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}{K}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\ex_{{x}}\left[\one\big\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}{K}}}}\notin\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}\big\}\left(\tau^{{\eta}}_{({n}+1){K}}-\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}{K}}\right)\right]

(D.6)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\ex_{{x}}\left[\one\big\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}{K}}}}\notin\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}\big\}\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{n}{K}}}}\left[\tau^{{\eta}}_{K}\right]}\right]\,,

where we used the strong Markov property in the last equality, since
\tau^{{\eta}}_{1}
is always finite almost surely by
Eq.

D.5
.

Combining
Eq.

D.6
with
Eq.

D.5
, we obtain the bound:

(D.7)

\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]=\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\ex_{{x}}\left[\one\big\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}}}\notin\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}\big\}\right]\leq\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\sum_{{n}=0}^{\infty}\ex_{{x}}\left[\one\big\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{n}}}\notin\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}\big\}\right]\times e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}=\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\times e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,,

which yields the result.
∎

Assumption 9
.

For any
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,

(D.8)

{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}<+\infty\,.

From now on, we will assume that
Assumption

9
holds.

Definition 13
.

For
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
, we define the following quantities:

(D.9)

\displaystyle{{E}}\left({i}\not\leadsto{\mathcal{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\min\left\{\sum_{{k}\to{l}\in{g}}{{{B}}}_{{k},{l}}:{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}\left({i}\not\leadsto{\mathcal{Q}}\right)\right\}

(D.10)

\displaystyle{{E}}({\mathcal{Q}})

\displaystyle\coloneqq\min\left\{\sum_{{k}\to{l}\in{g}}{{{B}}}_{{k},{l}}:{g}\in{\mathcal{G}}({\mathcal{Q}})\right\}

(D.11)

\displaystyle{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)

\displaystyle\coloneqq{{E}}({\mathcal{Q}})-{{E}}\left({i}\not\leadsto{\mathcal{Q}}\right)\,.

Note that
Assumption

9
ensures that all these quantities are finite.

Lemma D.3
.

For any
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
,

(D.12)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{x}}\left[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

Fix
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
. We will apply
Lemma

D.1
to the accelerated induced chain.

Let us first verify the assumptions of these lemmas. By
Lemmas

C.6
and
C.7
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{j}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}\}}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
:

(D.13)

\displaystyle\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

(D.14)

\displaystyle\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

Note that
Assumption

9
ensures that both
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
and
{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}
are finite.

We define, for any
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
,
{j}\in{\mathcal{V}}
with
{i}\neq{j}
:

(D.15)

\displaystyle{{p}}_{{i}{j}}\coloneqq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}\right)\quad\text{ and,}\quad{{p}}_{{i}{j}}\coloneqq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}\right)\,.

Let us verify the conditions of
Lemma

D.1
with
{a}\coloneqq e^{{\varepsilon}/{\eta}}
:

By
Eqs.

D.13
and
D.14
, for all
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
,
{j}\in{\mathcal{V}}
with
{i}\neq{j}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
:

(D.16)

{a}{{p}}_{{i}{j}}\leq\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})\leq{a}{{p}}_{{i}{j}}\,.

Now we can apply
Lemma

D.1
to obtain that for
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
:

(D.17)

{a}^{-3\times 4^{{r}}}e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq{a}^{3\times 4^{{r}}}e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)}{{\eta}}}\,.

Recalling that
{a}=e^{{\varepsilon}/{\eta}}
, the bounds in
Eq.

D.17
become:

(D.18)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-3\times 4^{{r}}{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)+3\times 4^{{r}}{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,,

which concludes the proof since
{\varepsilon}>0
was arbitrary.

∎

Definition 14
.

Let us now define, for
{x}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
,

(D.19)

\displaystyle{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{x}\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\max_{{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}}\left({{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-{{{B}}}_{{x}{i}}\right)\right]_{+}\,.

with the convention that these quantities are zero if
{x}\in\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}
or equal to the quantities defined in
Definition

13
if
{x}\in\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
.

Note that these quantities defined in
Definition

14
are non-negative and finite by
Assumption

9
.

Lemma D.4
.

For any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(D.20)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)-{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{x}}\left[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

If
{{x}}_{{0}}\in\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{K}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
, then
Lemma

D.3
applies and yields the result. if
{{x}}_{{0}}\in\bigcup_{{i}={K}+1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
, then the result holds trivially.
Let us now consider the general case where
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}\setminus\bigcup_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
and let us first prove the upper bound, the lower bound follows similarly.

By
Lemma

D.3
and
Lemma

C.13
:
for small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,
{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}
,
{x}^{\prime}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
, both

(D.21)

\ex_{{x}^{\prime}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)

and,

(D.22)

\accprobalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({{x}}_{{0}},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}})\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{{{B}}}_{{{x}}_{{0}},{i}}}{{\eta}}+\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)

hold.

Using the strong Markov property at time
\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}
(which is finite almost surely by
Corollary

C.1
), we have:

\displaystyle\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{{i}=1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left[(1+\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}}}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}])\one\left\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{{i}=1}^{{K}}\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left[\ex_{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}}}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\one\left\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}\right\}\right]+\sum_{{i}={K}+1}^{{N_{{\text{crit}}}}}0\times\one\left\{{x^{{\eta}}_{\tau^{{\eta}}_{{K}}}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}\right\}

(D.23)

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{{i}=1}^{{K}}\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-{{{B}}}_{{{x}}_{{0}},{i}}+2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

where we used
Eqs.

D.21
and
D.22
in the last inequality.
Bounding the sum as follows

\displaystyle 1+\sum_{{i}=1}^{{K}}\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-{{{B}}}_{{{x}}_{{0}},{i}}+2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq({K}+1)\exp\left(\frac{0\vee\max_{{i}\in{\{1,\dotsc,{K}\}}}\left({{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-{{{B}}}_{{{x}}_{{0}},{i}}+2{\varepsilon}\right)}{{\eta}}\right)

(D.24)

\displaystyle=({K}+1)\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+2{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)\,,

yields the upper bound.

The lower bound follows similarly using
Lemma

C.14
and the lower bound from
Lemma

D.3
.

∎

Theorem D.1
.

For any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(D.25)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)-{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

The result follows directly by combining
Lemmas

D.2
and
D.4
.

Specifically, by
Lemma

D.4
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
:

(D.26)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Then by
Lemma

D.2
, potentially reducing
{\eta}_{0}
, we have:

(D.27)

\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\times e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Combining these inequalities and using the fact that
{\varepsilon}>0
was arbitrary concludes the proof.
∎

The following lemma, though looking at first weaker than
Theorem

D.1
, will be useful later due to its uniformity in the initial condition in neighborhoods of the components.

Lemma D.5
.

Under
Assumption

9
, for any
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{{i}}
,

(D.28)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)-{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{x}}\left[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

The result follows directly by combining
Lemmas

D.2
and
D.3
.

Specifically, by
Lemma

D.3
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
:

(D.29)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{x}}\left[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{i}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Then by
Lemma

D.2
, potentially reducing
{\eta}_{0}
, we have:

(D.30)

\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\ex_{{x}}[\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\leq\ex_{{x}}[\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}]\times e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Combining these inequalities and using the fact that
{\varepsilon}>0
was arbitrary concludes the proof.
∎

Another technical lemma that will be useful later is the following.

Lemma D.6
.

For any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(D.31)

e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)-{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\leq\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left[\widetilde{\tau}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\right]

Proof.

This result follows directly from
Lemma

D.4
and the fact that
\widetilde{\tau}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\leq\widetilde{\tau}^{{K}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
.
∎

D.3.
Hitting time of
SGD

Leveraging the bounds on the hitting time of the accelerated process, we can now derive bounds on the hitting time of
SGD
. Denote by
\tau
the hitting time of
{\mathcal{V}}=\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}
for the
SGD
sequence:

(D.32)

\tau\coloneqq\inf\left\{{n}\geq{0}:{x}_{{n}}\in\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}\right\}\,.

It corresponds to the hitting time of
SGD
defined in the main text
Eq.

10
in the simpler case where each
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}
is of the form
{\mathcal{V}}_{j}={{\mathcal{U}}_{{\delta}}({\mathcal{C}}_{j})}
for
{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
.
We now state our first main results on the actual
SGD
sequence
Eq.

B.12
. The theorem below is a corollary of
Theorem

D.1
which concerned the subsampled process
Eq.

B.16
.

Theorem D.2
.

For any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for any
{\varepsilon}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(D.33)

\ex_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left[\tau\right]\leq e^{\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

This result follows from
Theorem

D.1
and the fact that, by construction,

(D.34)

\tau\leq\tau^{{\eta}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}\left\lceil{\eta}^{-1}\right\rceil\,,

with
\lceil{\eta}^{-1}\rceil\leq e^{\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}}
for small enough
{\eta}
.
∎

Corollary D.1
.

For any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for any
{\Delta}>0
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
,

(D.35)

\prob_{{x}}\left(\tau<\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\Delta}}{{\eta}}\right)\right)\leq e^{-\frac{{\Delta}}{2{\eta}}}\,.

Proof.

By Markov’s inequality and
Theorem

D.2
, for any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
:

(D.36)

\displaystyle\prob_{{x}}\left(\tau\geq\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\Delta}}{{\eta}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\ex_{{x}}\left[\tau\right]}{\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\Delta}}{{\eta}}\right)}

(D.37)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\Delta}/2}{{\eta}}\right)}{\exp\left(\frac{{{E}}\left({\mathcal{Q}}\,|\,{{x}}_{{0}}\right)+{\Delta}}{{\eta}}\right)}=\exp\left(-{\Delta}/2{\eta}\right)\,.

∎

Let us now focus on the lower-bound which requires more care. We will require the following additional assumption:

Assumption 10
.

Assume that there exist
{\mu}>0
,
{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}<+\infty
,
{R}>0
, such that,

(1)

\cl{\mathcal{U}}_{{R}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})
,
{j}=1,\dots,{N_{\text{targ}}}
are pairwise disjoint.

(2)

for all
{j}=1,\dots,{N_{\text{targ}}}
,
{x}\in\cl{\mathcal{U}}_{{R}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})
,

{u}({x},{\omega})
is a
{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}
-sub-Gaussian:

(D.38)

\forall{p}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}},\quad\log\ex\left[e^{\langle{p},{u}({x},{\omega})\rangle}\right]\leq\frac{{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2}\lVert{p}\rVert^{2}\,.

(3)

for all
{j}=1,\dots,{N_{\text{targ}}}
,
{x}\in\cl{\mathcal{U}}_{{R}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})
, there exists
{{{x}^{\prime}}}\in\proj_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}}({x})
a projection of
{x}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
such that:

(D.39)

\langle{\nabla}{f}({x}),{x}-{{{x}^{\prime}}}\rangle\geq\frac{{\mu}}{2}\lVert{x}-{{{x}^{\prime}}}\rVert^{2}

Note that, by
Assumption

2
and compactness of
\crit({f})
, such a
{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}
always exists since one can take

(D.40)

{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}=\sup\left\{{\sigma^{2}_{\infty}}({f}({x})):{x}\in\bigcup_{{j}=1}^{{N_{\text{targ}}}}\cl{\mathcal{U}}_{{R}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\right\}\,.

In particular, if
{\sigma^{2}_{\infty}}
is constant, then one can simply take
{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}={\sigma^{2}_{\infty}}
.

Lemma D.7
.

Under
Assumption

10
, for any
{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
satisfying
d({{x}}_{{0}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\leq\frac{{R}}{12}
, we have, for
SGD
started at
{{x}}_{{0}}
:

(D.41)

\prob_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left(\forall{n}\geq{0},\,\,\,d({x}_{n},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\leq{R}\right)\geq 1-\exp\left(-\frac{{\mu}{R}^{2}}{1152{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}{\eta}}\right)\,.

Proof.

Let us denote by
{\mathcal{K}}\coloneqq\cl{\mathcal{U}}_{{R}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{j})

Given
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{\mathcal{K}}
,
we define the projected
SGD
sequence as

(D.42)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &{{p}}_{{0}}={{x}}_{{0}}\\
&{{p}}_{{n}+1}\in\proj_{\mathcal{K}}\left({{p}_{{n}}}-{\eta}{\nabla}{f}({{p}_{{n}}})+{\eta}{{u}_{{n}}}\right)\,,\quad\text{ where }{{u}_{{n}}}={u}({{p}_{{n}}},{{\omega}_{{n}}})\,.\end{aligned}\right.

By
Assumption

10
, for any
{n}\geq{0}
, there exists
{{y}_{{n}}}\in\proj_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}}({{p}_{{n}}})
such that:

(D.43)

\langle{\nabla}{f}({{p}_{{n}}}),{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rangle\geq\frac{{\mu}}{2}\lVert{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rVert^{2}\,.

Define the martingale sequence
({M}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
by:

(D.44)

{M}_{n}\coloneqq\sum_{{{k}}=0}^{n}(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{{n}-{{k}}}\langle{u}_{{k}},{p}_{{k}}-{y}_{{k}}\rangle\,.

We recursively compute the moment generating function of
{M}_{n}
: for
{\alpha}\geq 0
,
{n}\geq{0}
, by the tower property of conditional expectation and
Assumption

10
:

\displaystyle\ex\left[e^{{\alpha}{M}_{n}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}^{2}{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2}\right)\exp\left(\frac{(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{2}{\alpha}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2}\right)\dots\exp\left(\frac{(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{2{n}}{\alpha}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}^{2}{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2}\times\frac{1-(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{2({n}+1)}}{1-(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{2}}\right)

(D.45)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}^{2}{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2{\mu}{\eta}}\right)\,,

provided that
{\eta}
is small enough so that
{\mu}{\eta}<1
. Note that we used that the iterates of
Eq.

D.42
are at distance at most
{R}
from
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
by construction.

Since
\left(e^{{\alpha}{M}_{n}}\right)_{{n}\geq{0}}
is a convex function of the martingale sequence
\left({M}_{n}\right)_{{n}\geq{0}}
, it is a sub-martingale.
We can apply Doob’s maximal inequality to the non-negative sub-martingale
({M}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
to obtain that for any
{\Delta}>0
,
{N}\geq{0}
:

\displaystyle\prob\left(\sup_{{n}\leq{N}-1}{M}_{n}\geq{\Delta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\prob\left(\sup_{{n}\leq{N}-1}e^{{\alpha}{M}_{n}}\geq e^{{\alpha}{\Delta}}\right)

(D.46)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{{\alpha}^{2}{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}{2{\mu}{\eta}}-{\alpha}{\Delta}\right)\,,

where we used
Eq.

D.45
in the last inequality.
Optimizing the right-hand side of
Eq.

D.46
with respect to
{\alpha}
and setting
{\alpha}=\frac{{\mu}{\eta}{\Delta}}{{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}
, we obtain:

(D.47)

\prob\left(\sup_{{n}\leq{N}-1}{M}_{n}\geq{\Delta}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{\mu}{\eta}{\Delta}^{2}}{2{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}\right)\,.

Taking
{\Delta}\leftarrow{\Delta}/{\eta}
and
{N}\to+\infty
, we obtain that, by monotone continuity of probability measures, for any
{\Delta}>0
:

(D.48)

\prob\left(\sup_{{n}\geq{0}}{\eta}{M}_{n}\geq{\Delta}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{{\mu}{\Delta}^{2}}{2{\eta}{R}^{2}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}}\right)\,.

Denote by
{G}\coloneqq\sup_{{x}\in{\mathcal{K}},{\omega}\in{\Omega}}\lVert-{\nabla}{f}({x})+{u}({x},{\omega})\rVert
the bound on the gradient and on the noise on
{\mathcal{K}}
, which is finite by
Assumptions

5
and
7
.

Let us now derive a recursive inequality for the distance to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{j}
. For any iterate
{n}\geq{0}
, by non-expansiveness of the projection, we have:

\displaystyle d^{2}({{p}}_{{n}+1},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq\lVert{{p}}_{{n}+1}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert{{p}_{{n}}}-{\eta}{\nabla}{f}({{p}_{{n}}})+{\eta}{{u}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\lVert{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rVert^{2}+{\eta}^{2}\lVert{\nabla}{f}({{p}_{{n}}})-{{u}_{{n}}}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-2{\eta}\langle{\nabla}{f}({{p}_{{n}}}),{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rangle+2{\eta}\langle{{u}_{{n}}},{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rangle

(D.49)

\displaystyle\leq(1-{\mu}{\eta})\lVert{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rVert^{2}+{\eta}^{2}{G}^{2}+2{\eta}\langle{{u}_{{n}}},{{p}_{{n}}}-{{y}_{{n}}}\rangle\,,

where we used
Eq.

D.43
in the last inequality.

Iterating this inequality and using that
d({p}_{n},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})=\lVert{p}_{n}-{y}_{n}\rVert
, we obtain:

\displaystyle d^{2}({p}_{n},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{n}d^{2}({{x}}_{{0}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})+{\eta}^{2}{G}^{2}\sum_{i=0}^{{n}-1}(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{i}

\displaystyle\quad+2{\eta}\sum_{i=0}^{{n}-1}(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{{n}-1-i}\langle{u}_{i},{p}_{i}-{y}_{i}\rangle

\displaystyle=(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{n}d^{2}({{x}}_{{0}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})+{\eta}^{2}{G}^{2}\frac{1-(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{n}}{{\mu}{\eta}}+2{\eta}{M}_{{n}-1}

(D.50)

\displaystyle\leq(1-{\mu}{\eta})^{n}d^{2}({{x}}_{{0}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})+\frac{2{\eta}{G}^{2}}{{\mu}}+2{\eta}{M}_{{n}-1}\,.

Using that
d({{x}}_{{0}},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\leq\frac{{R}}{12}
by assumption, requiring that
{\eta}>0
be small enough so that
\frac{2{\eta}{G}^{2}}{{\mu}}\leq\frac{{R}^{2}}{12}
and taking
{\Delta}\leftarrow\frac{{R}^{2}}{24}
in
Eq.

D.48
, we obtain that for any
{n}\geq{0}
, with probability at least
1-\exp\left(-\frac{{\mu}{R}^{2}}{1152{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}{\eta}}\right)
:

(D.51)

d^{2}({p}_{n},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\leq\frac{{R}^{2}}{4}\,.

In addition, take
{\eta}>0
small enough so that
\frac{{R}}{2}+{\eta}{G}\leq\frac{{R}}{2}
: this implies that if
{x}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
so that
d({x},{\mathcal{Q}}_{j})\leq\frac{{R}}{2}
, then
{x}-{\eta}{\nabla}{f}({x})+{\eta}{u}({x},{\omega})\in{\mathcal{K}}
. Combining this remark with
Eq.

D.51
, we can show recursively that the sequences
({x}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
and
({p}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
coincide with probability at least
1-\exp\left(-\frac{{\mu}{R}^{2}}{1152{\overline{\sigma}^{2}_{\infty}}{\eta}}\right)
, yielding the desired result.

∎

Define by
\widetilde{\tau}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}
the hitting time of
\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{\mathcal{V}}_{{j}}
by the induced chain
({{z}}_{n})_{{n}\geq{0}}
(see
Definition

8
).

Lemma D.8
.

Define

(D.52)

{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{\infty}}\coloneqq\max_{{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}}\min_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}+1\,.

For any
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
, for
{\mathcal{V}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{V}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
neighborhoods of
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{C}}_{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
small enough, there is
{{\eta}}_{{0}}>0
such that for all
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
, for any
{n}\geq{0}
,

(D.53)

\prob_{{{x}}_{{0}}}\left(\widetilde{\tau}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}>{n}\right)\leq\left(1-e^{-\frac{{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{\infty}}}{{\eta}}}\right)^{{n}}\,.

Note that
Assumption

9
ensures that
{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{\infty}}
is finite.

Proof.

Fix some
{{x}}_{{0}}\in{{{\mathbb{R}}}^{{{d}}}}
.
By definition of
{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{\infty}}
, for any vertex
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
, there exists
{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}
such that:

(D.54)

{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}\leq{{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{\infty}}-1\,.

Fix
{\varepsilon}\leftarrow 1
. By
Lemma

C.4
, for small enough neighborhoods
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{i}
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{i}
,
{i}=1,\dots,{N_{{\text{crit}}}}
, there exists
{\eta}_{0}>0
such that for all
{i}\in{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{Q}}
,
{x}\in{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{i}}
,
0<{\eta}<{\eta}_{0}
:

\displaystyle\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}\left({x},\bigcup_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\max_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}\probalt_{{{\mathcal{V}}}}({x},{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{j}})

\displaystyle\geq\exp\left(-\frac{\min_{{j}\in{\mathcal{Q}}}{{{\mathcal{B}}}}_{{i},{j}}}{{\eta}}-\frac{{\varepsilon}}{{\eta}}\right)

(D.55)
